=== PROMPT VARIANT ===
r1

=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
You are given integers N, M and a length-N sequence of non-negative integers A = (A_1, A_2, \ldots, A_N).
For k = 0, 1, \ldots, M-1, solve the following problem:

Define an integer sequence B = (B_1, B_2, \ldots, B_N) so that B_i is the remainder of A_i + k when divided by M. Find the inversion number in B.


What is the inversion number?
The inversion number of a sequence (A_1, A_2, \dots, A_N) is the number of integer pairs (i, j) satisfying 1 \le i < j \le N and A_i > A_j.

Input

The input is given from Standard Input in the following format:
N M
A_1 A_2 \ldots A_N

Output

Print M lines.
The i-th line (1 \le i \le M) should contain the answer for the case k = i-1.

Constraints


- 1 \le N,M \le 2\times 10^5
- 0 \le A_i < M
- All input values are integers.

Sample Input 1

3 3
2 1 0

Sample Output 1

3
1
1


- For k=0: B=(2, 1, 0). The inversion number is 3.
- For k=1: B=(0, 2, 1). The inversion number is 1.
- For k=2: B=(1, 0, 2). The inversion number is 1.

Sample Input 2

5 6
5 3 5 0 1

Sample Output 2

7
3
3
1
1
5

Sample Input 3

7 7
0 1 2 3 4 5 6

Sample Output 3

0
6
10
12
12
10
6

Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

This relation depends on k relative to a,b.

Maybe inversion count for B can be expressed as base inv(A) plus changes when adding k? Let's examine.

Let A_i values. Adding k modulo M maps values: values < M-k become +k; values >= M-k wrap to +k-M (small). This is a cyclic shift of value order. The inversion count of sequence after applying a monotone-ish transformation to each value. Need all cyclic shifts.

Could use data structures.

Let's define for fixed k, B_i = A_i + k if A_i < M-k, else A_i + k - M. Thus transformation is piecewise: low values increase by k, high values wrap to low. It is not globally monotone: it maps [0, M-k-1] to [k, M-1] increasing, and [M-k, M-1] to [0,k-1] increasing. So B sequence is original A with values in two intervals shifted to top and bottom.

Inversion count can be computed from original inversion count plus cross effects between low/high groups? Maybe.

Let L = {i: A_i < M-k} (non-wrapped), H = {i: A_i >= M-k} (wrapped). For k=0, H empty? M-k = M, A_i < M all L.

For k>0:
- Within L: values transformed by +k, order preserved, so inversions among L unchanged.
- Within H: values transformed by +k-M, order preserved, so inversions among H unchanged.
- Cross pairs between L and H:
   * i in L, j in H: original A_i in [0,M-k-1], A_j in [M-k,M-1], so A_i < A_j originally, no inversion. After shift: B_i in [k,M-1], B_j in [0,k-1], so B_i > B_j always? Since B_i >= k, B_j <= k-1, yes inversion. Thus all L-before-H pairs become inversions.
   * i in H, j in L: original A_i > A_j (since H values >= M-k > L values), so original inversion. After shift: B_i in [0,k-1], B_j in [k,M-1], so B_i < B_j, no inversion. Thus all H-before-L inversions disappear.
So for k>0:
inv(B(k)) = inv(A) + (# pairs i<j with A_i in L, A_j in H) - (# pairs i<j with A_i in H, A_j in L).
Wait within L/H unchanged, cross L-H all become inversion, cross H-L none. Original cross L-H no inversion, H-L inversion. So formula.

This is promising. Let threshold T = M-k. L values < T, H values >= T. Need for each k (or T) compute:

Y(T) = # pairs i<j with A_i >= T and A_j < T.

X(T) counts ordered pairs (i<j) where first value < T, second value >= T.
Y(T) counts ordered pairs where first value >= T, second value < T.

Need maybe include `sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()` for speed. Code:
```
import sys

def main():
    data = list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    if not data:
        return
    N, M = data[0], data[1]
    A = data[2:]
    bit = [0]*(M+1)
    sum_w = [0]*M
    inv = 0
    for idx, x in enumerate(A, 1):
        # count <= x
        s = 0
        i = x + 1
        while i > 0:
            s += bit[i]
            i -= i & -i
        inv += idx - 1 - s
        i = x + 1
        while i <= M:
            bit[i] += 1
            i += i & -i
        sum_w[x] += N - 2 * idx + 1
    D = [0]*(M+1)
    cur = 0
    for T in range(1, M+1):
        cur += sum_w[T-1]
        D[T] = cur
    out = [str(inv + D[M - k]) for k in range(M)]
    sys.stdout.write("\n".join(out))

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```

Let's double-check D identity with pair contribution and weights:
D(T)=Σ_{i<j}(C_i-C_j). For each position p with C_p=1, its contribution to D? Let's derive in another way:

D = Σ_{i<j}(C_i - C_j) = Σ_{i<j} C_i - Σ_{i<j} C_j.
Σ_{i<j} C_i = Σ_i C_i * (N - i) (number of j>i).
Σ_{i<j} C_j = Σ_j C_j * (j - 1) (number of i<j).

Let's implement with data list:
```
def main():
    import sys
    data = list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    N = data[0]; M = data[1]
    bit = [0]*(M+1)
    sum_w = [0]*M
    inv = 0
    for idx in range(1, N+1):
        x = data[idx+1]
        # Fenwick query
        s = 0
        i = x + 1
        while i > 0:
            s += bit[i]
            i -= i & -i
        inv += idx - 1 - s
        i = x + 1
        while i <= M:
            bit[i] += 1
            i += i & -i
        sum_w[x] += N - 2 * idx + 1
    D = [0]*(M+1)
    cur = 0
    for T in range(1, M+1):
        cur += sum_w[T-1]
        D[T] = cur
    out = [str(inv + D[M-k]) for k in range(M)]
    sys.stdout.write("\n".join(out))
```
Check indexing: data length = 2+N. For idx=N, data[N+1] = last A. Good.

Let's examine the derivation of ans = inv0 + D(T) more rigorously:
For fixed k, T=M-k. Define L = {i | A_i < T}, H = {i | A_i >= T}. If k=0, T=M, H empty; formula still works because L all, H empty. For k>0:
- If both in L: B_i=A_i+k, B_j=A_j+k, so order same.
- If both in H: B_i=A_i+k-M, B_j=A_j+k-M, order same.
- If i in L, j in H: B_i in [k,M-1], B_j in [0,k-1], so inversion regardless. Original A_i < T <= A_j, so no inversion.
- If i in H, j in L: B_i in [0,k-1], B_j in [k,M-1], so no inversion. Original A_i >= T > A_j, so inversion.
Thus inv(B)=inv(A)+X-Y. X-Y = D(T). Good.

D(T) = Σ_{i<j}(C_i - C_j) with C_i = 1 if A_i<T. Then:
Σ_{i<j}(C_i - C_j) = Σ_i C_i * ((N-i) - (i-1)) = Σ_i C_i (N - 2i +1). Because for each i with C_i=1, it contributes +1 for each j>i with C_j=0? Wait algebra:
Σ_{i<j}(C_i - C_j) = Σ_i C_i * (N - i) - Σ_j C_j * (j -1). Combine over same index p: C_p[(N-p) - (p-1)] = C_p(N-2p+1). Yes. So D(T)=Σ_{p:A_p<T}(N-2p+1). Good.

Thus code.

Let's decide final code style:
```
import sys

def main():
    data = list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    if not data:
        return
    N, M = data[0], data[1]

    bit = [0] * (M + 1)
    sum_w = [0] * M
    inv = 0

    for idx in range(1, N + 1):
        x = data[idx + 1]

        # Fenwick: number of previous elements <= x
        s = 0
        i = x + 1
        while i > 0:
            s += bit[i]
            i -= i & -i
        inv += idx - 1 - s

        i = x + 1
        while i <= M:
            bit[i] += 1
            i += i & -i

        sum_w[x] += N - 2 * idx + 1

    # D[T] = sum of weights of positions with A_i < T
    D = [0] * (M + 1)
    cur = 0
    for T in range(1, M + 1):
        cur += sum_w[T - 1]
        D[T] = cur

    out = [str(inv + D[M - k]) for k in range(M)]
    sys.stdout.write("\n".join(out))

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```
Let's check indexing for data: data[0]=N, data[1]=M, data[2]=A1. For idx=1, data[idx+1]=data[2]. For idx=N, data[N+1] = last A. Good. If N=1, data length3, idx=1 data2. Good.

Now, let's perhaps think about if there is a possible problem with using `sum_w` prefix D but not considering positions order within same value? The formula D(T)=Σ_{p:A_p<T}(N-2p+1) is exact; no issue.

Let's derive D(T) from pair contributions to be absolutely certain:
D(T)=X-Y = Σ_{i<j} [A_i<T<=A_j] - [A_i>=T>A_j].
Consider each position p with A_p<T (C_p=1). In pairs (i,p) with i<p:
- If A_i<T (C_i=1), term C_i-C_p =0.
- If A_i>=T (C_i=0), term -1. This corresponds to pair (i,p) with i in H, p in L, Y contribution. There are (p-1 - #previous ones) such? Wait in sum over all pairs of C_i-C_j, for a fixed p as second, contribution is C_i - C_p = C_i -1, which is -1 if C_i=0, 0 if C_i=1. So contribution from pairs before p = - (# zeros before p). In pairs (p,j) with j>p:
- C_p - C_j = 1 - C_j, contribution +1 if C_j=0, 0 if C_j=1. So + (# zeros after p).
Thus total D = Σ_{p:C_p=1} (zeros_after_p - zeros_before_p). Is this equal to Σ_{p:C_p=1}(N-2p+1)? Wait zeros_after_p - zeros_before_p depends on positions of zeros, not just p. Let's test with ones at positions 1,2 in N=3. For p1: zeros_after=1 (pos3), zeros_before=0 =>1. p2: zeros_after=1, zeros_before=0 =>1. Sum=2. N-2p+1: p1=2, p2=0 sum2. They are equal but not termwise. So the algebra via Σ_i C_i(N-i) - Σ_j C_j(j-1) is correct:
D = Σ_{i<j} C_i - Σ_{i<j} C_j = Σ_i C_i*(N-i) - Σ_j C_j*(j-1) = Σ_p C_p*(N-p - p +1) = Σ_p C_p*(N-2p+1). This is exact. Good. The "zeros_after - zeros_before" sum equals this by identity. So no issue.

Let's test with ones at positions 1,3 in N=4. C=[1,0,1,0]. D pairs:
(1,2):1, (1,3):0, (1,4):1, (2,3):-1, (2,4):0, (3,4):1 =>2? Sum =1+0+1-1+0+1=2.
Formula weights p1=4-2+1=3, p3=4-6+1=-1 sum2. Good. zeros_after-before: p1 zeros_after2 before0=2; p3 zeros_after1 before1=0 sum2. Good.

Thus D prefix by weights works.

Let's maybe write final code with comments:
```
import sys

def main():
    data = list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    if not data:
        return
    N, M = data[0], data[1]

    bit = [0] * (M + 1)
    weight_sum = [0] * M

    inv0 = 0
    for idx in range(1, N + 1):
        x = data[idx + 1]

        # count previous elements <= x
        s = 0
        i = x + 1
        while i > 0:
            s += bit[i]
            i -= i & -i
        inv0 += idx - 1 - s

        i = x + 1
        while i <= M:
            bit[i] += 1
            i += i & -i

        weight_sum[x] += N - 2 * idx + 1

    # D[t] = sum of weights of positions with A_i < t
    D = [0] * (M + 1)
    cur = 0
    for t in range(1, M + 1):
        cur += weight_sum[t - 1]
        D[t] = cur

    ans = [str(inv0 + D[M - k]) for k in range(M)]
    sys.stdout.write("\n".join(ans))

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```

=== ANSWER ===
```python
import sys

def main():
    data = list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    if not data:
        return

    N, M = data[0], data[1]

    bit = [0] * (M + 1)
    weight_sum = [0] * M

    inv0 = 0

    for idx in range(1, N + 1):
        x = data[idx + 1]

        # Count previous elements <= x
        s = 0
        i = x + 1
        while i > 0:
            s += bit[i]
            i -= i & -i

        inv0 += idx - 1 - s

        # Add x to Fenwick tree
        i = x + 1
        while i <= M:
            bit[i] += 1
            i += i & -i

        # Weight of this position for D(T)
        weight_sum[x] += N - 2 * idx + 1

    # D[T] = sum of weights of positions with A_i < T
    D = [0] * (M + 1)
    cur = 0
    for T in range(1, M + 1):
        cur += weight_sum[T - 1]
        D[T] = cur

    ans = [str(inv0 + D[M - k]) for k in range(M)]
    sys.stdout.write("\n".join(ans))

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```
